a:T71f,Several design choices matter. The source data is not arbitrary English web text but the actual high-quality split of Nemotron-CC, chosen because prior baseline work found it to be one of the strongest open English pretraining sources. This is important: by starting from a strong source corpus, the paper avoids conflating source-data noise with translation quality. The authors uniformly sample about 155 million documents, around 100B English tokens, and translate the same source set into each target language, which naturally creates a multi-way parallel corpus. On the translation side, they do not rely on a single model family. Instead they optimize over quality, throughput, and language coverage: Tower+ 9B and 72B for stronger-coverage higher-resource settings, and OPUS-MT/HPLT-MT for broader lower-resource coverage. Before production, they run a small human fluency study over seven languages and six candidate systems using blind ratings on matched source documents. Tower+ ranks highest, while general-purpose instruction LLMs lag clearly behind, so those are removed from the production pool. That decision matters because one of the paper’s later findings is that translation-system quality does affect downstream pretraining, even when standard benchmarks fail to expose it. The corpus preserves source-document alignment and, for some languages, multiple row-aligned translations from different systems. This makes the resource useful not only for per-language pretraining but also for controlled cross-system and cross-language experiments. The training setup is also fairly disciplined: reference LLMs are trained under matched budgets on translated data and native baselines, then compared on token efficiency, MT-system choice, benchmark blind spots, and phenomenon-specific Norwegian evaluations.b:T756,Four result groups stand out. First is scale and coverage. MultiSynt/MT spans 36 languages and about 4.8T target-language tokens; for many lower- and medium-resource European languages it exceeds the largest comparable open native resource by more than an order of magnitude. Second is pretraining efficiency. On standard multilingual base-model benchmarks, models trained on MultiSynt/MT reach the final score of the HPLT 2.0 native-data baseline using about 72% fewer training tokens, and at a matched 100B-token budget they outperform it by roughly 15% relative. That is a strong indication that translated data can be highly information-dense. Third is evaluation blindness. A fluency-sensitive LLM-as-judge setup cleanly recovers the ranking of translation systems, while standard multiple-choice benchmarks largely fail to separate them. An embedding-space nearest-neighbor analysis further shows that benchmark items tend to sit closer to MultiSynt/MT documents than to HPLT documents, suggesting that benchmark distribution alignment may inflate the apparent strength of translated-data pretraining. Fourth is capability boundary analysis. On Norwegian tasks involving idioms and culturally grounded knowledge, native-data models remain better throughout training. On Norwegian commonsense reasoning, however, translated-data models close the gap and eventually overtake the native baseline. This is one of the most useful findings in the paper because it decomposes the usual criticism of translationese into something operational: translated data is not uniformly worse, but it is weaker on local cultural grounding and idiomatic expression. The overlap experiment between Tower+ 72B and 9B also matters: the downstream gap is small enough that 9B becomes the default production choice, which is relevant for cost-quality trade-offs in large-scale corpus construction.c:T5ab,A good reading order is data construction, then training results, then evaluation critique. Start with Section 3. The important parts are not the mechanics of translation at scale but the choices: why Nemotron-CC HQ was used as the source, why the final production pool combines Tower+ with OPUS/HPLT-MT, and why preserving parallel alignment matters for later controlled analysis. Then read Section 4 carefully, since this is where the pretraining value is established. Pay attention to how token efficiency is measured, whether budgets are matched fairly, and how much downstream difference remains between translation systems. After that, read Section 5 with care, because it changes how the earlier gains should be interpreted. Section 5.1 compares LLM-as-judge with standard benchmarks, Section 5.2 gives the embedding-space diagnostic, and Section 5.3 breaks down Norwegian performance into cultural knowledge, idioms, and commonsense. Together they show that benchmark gains should not be read as proof that translated corpora fully replace native corpora. If time is limited, prioritize Figure 1, Figure 3, Figure 6, and the main tables in Sections 4 and 5. Two deeper questions are worth carrying forward: whether the best recipe is a translated-native mixture rather than either source alone, and how multilingual evaluation should separate fluency, cultural grounding, and reasoning instead of collapsing them into a single benchmark score.d:T959,方法核心是把数据配比优化写成带协变量的连续处理因果推断问题。每次 proxy 训练 run 记为三元组 (X, T, Y)：X 是训练前可观测的数据状态，包含质量、难度、复杂度、风格等统计特征；T 是各域 mixture；Y 是训练后的下游性能。作者强调 X 不能包含任何训练后模型信息或评测结果，否则会破坏因果设定。

处理变量 T 不是直接用 simplex 上的原始比例，而是先做 log-mixture 变换 z=log(t+ε)。这样做有两个原因：一是经验 scaling law 常表现为边际收益递减，log 空间更适合局部线性近似；二是 compositional data 在 simplex 上直接回归不稳定，Aitchison 风格的几何处理更自然。随后作者不去拟合完整响应面 μ(x,z)，而是在历史 mixture 支持域附近采用 partially linear approximation：Y≈g(X)+θ(X)^T z+noise。这里 g(X) 表示由数据状态决定的基线性能，θ(X) 是状态条件化的边际数据回报向量。θ_k(X)>0 表示在当前数据状态下提高第 k 个域占比通常有利，θ_k(X)<0 则意味着可能存在负迁移或技能冲突。

为了从观测到的历史 run 中识别 θ(X)，作者使用 Double Machine Learning 和 causal forest。具体思路是先分别拟合 outcome model 和 treatment model，对 Y 与 T 关于 X 的可预测部分做 orthogonalization / residualization，再在残差空间里估计局部线性效应。这样做的目的是把“数据池本身更好，所以性能更高”与“某个域比例更高，所以性能更高”分开，减少混杂偏差。得到 CATE 估计后，作者不是直接跳到新的最优配比，而是做 trust-region 约束下的保守更新，避免由于局部估计噪声把 mixture 一步推到训练支持域之外。这一点很关键，因为历史 proxy run 只有有限覆盖，过度外推很容易失真。

与 RegMix 一类方法相比，差别不在于用了更复杂的回归器，而在于目标函数变了。RegMix 更像学习一个静态全局 surrogate；CausalMix 学的是“在给定数据状态下，沿哪个方向改 mixture 更可能带来真实收益”。这使它天然更适合数据池变化、模型规模迁移和解释分析。论文还把 CATE 用于分析域间相互作用，而不是只输出一个配比建议，这让方法从纯黑盒调参器变成了可诊断工具。e:T9f8,CausalMix formulates each proxy training run as a triplet (X, T, Y), where X is the pre-training data state, T is the domain mixture, and Y is the downstream performance after training. A key modeling choice is that X contains only pre-training statistics of the data pool, such as quality, difficulty, complexity, entropy, or style, and explicitly excludes any post-training model signal or evaluation outcome.

The treatment is not modeled directly on the simplex. Instead, the paper uses a log-mixture representation z = log(t + epsilon). This choice is motivated by two considerations: empirical diminishing returns are often closer to linear in log space, and compositional data are better handled with geometry-aware transforms than with raw simplex coordinates. Rather than fitting the full response surface mu(x, z), the paper adopts a partially linear local approximation, Y ≈ g(X) + theta(X)^T z + noise. Here g(X) captures the baseline performance implied by the data state, while theta(X) is the state-conditioned marginal return vector. Positive theta_k(X) means increasing domain k is locally beneficial under state X; negative values indicate local negative transfer or domain conflict.

To identify theta(X) from observational proxy runs, the paper uses Double Machine Learning and causal forests. The idea is to first model the predictable parts of both outcome and treatment from X, then residualize them and estimate the local linear effect in the orthogonalized space. This is the crucial step: it tries to disentangle “this pool performs better because the pool itself is better” from “this pool performs better because the mixture changed.” Once the conditional effect is estimated, the method performs a conservative trust-region update rather than jumping directly to an unconstrained optimum. That design is sensible because historical proxy runs only cover a limited region of the mixture space, and aggressive extrapolation would be unreliable.

Compared with methods such as RegMix, the main difference is not just a stronger regressor. The target itself changes. RegMix-style methods learn a static global surrogate from mixture to performance. CausalMix instead estimates a local, state-conditioned causal direction for how to move the mixture under the current pool. That makes it more suitable for distribution shift, transfer across model scales, and interpretation. The paper also uses the estimated CATEs to analyze domain interactions, which is more informative than returning a single recommended mixture.f:T5de,The core experimental setup uses 512 proxy runs of Qwen2.5-0.5B to fit the causal model, then tests whether the learned update rule transfers to unseen data pools and larger model architectures. From the provided text, these are the only hard numbers that can be confirmed directly: 512 runs and a 0.5B proxy model. The central empirical claim is that, unlike static-distribution mixture optimizers, CausalMix does not require retraining the proxy search from scratch when the data pool changes.

The reported findings, as described in the paper text, fall into three buckets. First, transfer: the learned causal update generalizes to unseen pools and larger models. Second, interpretability: the estimated conditional treatment effects reveal a conflict between factual-knowledge-heavy data and complex logical reasoning, and show that math and coding data become beneficial only above certain quality thresholds. Third, optimization behavior: trust-region updates appear more stable than aggressive reweighting, which is consistent with the local nature of the estimator.

What still needs to be checked in the full results section are the decisive quantitative details: which baselines were compared, the absolute and relative gains, how large the transfer models are, which data-state features matter most, and how much each component contributes in ablations. In particular, the necessity of DML, causal forests, log-mixture parameterization, and trust-region updates should be verified carefully.10:T659,This one is worth a close read. Start with the methodology and keep the three variables straight: X is the data state, T is the mixture, and Y is post-training performance. Once that framing is clear, the rest of the paper becomes much easier to parse. The next thing to focus on is why the paper estimates local marginal returns theta(X) instead of fitting a global response surface. That is the conceptual center of the work. If you have worked on data mixture or curriculum learning, the key insight is not the causal vocabulary by itself, but the explicit separation between pool-state effects and mixture effects.

In the experiments, prioritize four parts: the main transfer results on seen versus unseen pools and across model scales; the ablations removing data-state features, DML, trust-region constraints, or replacing the method with plain regression; the CATE visualizations supporting the skill-conflict and quality-threshold claims; and any case studies showing how the recommended mixture shifts under different pool states. Those sections will tell you whether the method is merely plausible or actually robust.

If time is limited, read Figure 1, the method overview, the main result tables, and the interpretability figures first. The most interesting follow-up questions are whether the chosen data-state features remain stable enough for real deployment, whether the same framework can scale from SFT to pretraining, whether the local linear assumption survives with more domains and sparser support, and whether this causal optimizer can be combined with online data selection or mid-training annealing.11:T8cf,The core technical scheme consists of four parts, with the key difference from prior methods being full adaptation to GRPO’s critic-free structure and the current RLHF setup without explicit off-policy corrections: 1. Behavior-policy-aware GRPO surrogate objective reconstruction: Unlike traditional GRPO derivations that assume equivalence between the behavior policy and the current learner policy, it explicitly disentangles the stale behavior policy φ (used to generate rollouts) from the learner parameter θ, and defines the surrogate gradient mapping ℋ(θ,φ) to avoid conflating the surrogate gradient with the total derivative of the distribution-dependent population objective, providing a rigorous foundation for lag bias analysis. 2. Per-step staleness bias quantification: Under three assumptions (local boundedness, distributional smoothness, behavior-policy smoothness), it strictly proves that the per-step surrogate gradient bias induced by stale rollouts scales as O(Sη), where S is the maximum rollout lag step count and η is the learning rate — meaning the single-step training safe region is controlled by the product Sη. 3. Conditional collapse-time scaling law: It distinguishes two types of constraints: the batch-level clipping radius from GRPO’s importance sampling ratio, and the learner drift threshold over the training horizon. Training stability is divided into two regimes: when Sη is small enough that per-step staleness bias stays within the tolerance of batch clipping, the collapse time follows a Tη horizon law (T is the total training step count), and the maximum stable learning rate is nearly independent of S; when Sη exceeds the batch clipping tolerance, stability is directly bounded by Sη. 4. Two-constraint stability rule: Combining the two regimes, it provides an actionable hyperparameter configuration formula η ≪ min{R_batch/(S·G_upd), R_crit/(T·G_upd)}, where R_batch is the batch-level clipping radius, R_crit is the critical drift threshold, and G_upd is the per-step gradient magnitude. This explains the industrial observation that the maximum stable learning rate sometimes appears independent of lag magnitude, which only occurs in scenarios dominated by the horizon constraint.12:T5a9,Prioritize the following sections for intensive reading: 1. Introduction: Focus on the throughput motivation of asynchronous GRPO, the core limitations of existing analyses, and the observational background of the two-phase "stable-then-collapse" training dynamic. 2. Behavior-policy-aware GRPO surrogate reconstruction: Focus on the definition logic of ℋ(θ,φ), and clarify the core reason why traditional GRPO derivations cannot handle staleness. 3. Per-step staleness bias proof: Pay attention to the applicability boundaries of the three assumptions — for example, when policy updates are overly aggressive, the local boundedness assumption fails and the conclusions no longer hold. 4. Conditional collapse-time scaling law derivation: Focus on the switching condition between the two regimes, i.e., the tradeoff logic between the batch-level clipping constraint and the horizon drift constraint. 5. If the full experimental section is available later: Prioritize training stability curves under different lag step counts S and learning rate η settings to verify the two-phase dynamic and the validity of the two-constraint rule. Worth further exploration: extending the framework to GRPO scenarios with universally added KL regularization, adapting it to multimodal RLHF setups, and combining it with explicit off-policy correction methods such as V-trace to increase the maximum allowable lag step count S for higher training throughput.13:T488,The core contribution is the mechanistic interpretation of "Muon as an implicit residual connection", which complements existing mathematical perspectives: the core function of residual connections is to provide downstream layers with both preserved original input and transformed representations via explicit orthogonal (identity mapping is a special case of orthogonal matrices) skip paths; the core operation of Muon is to replace the weight update ΔW with its nearest orthogonal matrix Orth(ΔW), which is equivalent to implicitly injecting orthogonal transformation updates into the optimization trajectory — the two follow the same logic, with the only difference being that residuals are explicit architectural operations while Muon’s effect is an implicit optimization-level operation. The work further analyzes the tradeoff in controlled linear optimization settings: orthogonal updates sacrifice a small amount of local target fitting speed (since updates do not strictly follow the gradient direction) but better preserve the structural properties of representations, improving the efficiency of downstream layer utilization of representations.14:T4ee,Message Passing Language Models (MPLMs) are implemented as a runtime framework rather than a new model:
- A single user query spawns multiple concurrent LLM threads, each with its own prompt/context and textual ID, decoded in parallel via batched inference.
- The model emits tagged substrings that the runtime interprets as execution directives, analogous to MPI primitives:
  - <spawn[...]> ... </spawn>: create new threads with given IDs and seed prompts.
  - <send[...]> ... </send>: send a text message to specified threads.
  - <recv[...]>: block until messages arrive from specified threads.
  - <stop>: terminate a thread.
- A lightweight controller sits above the inference engine, handling scheduling, message queues, blocking/unblocking, and injecting received messages into the target thread contexts—without changing token sampling or KV-cache management.
- Key difference vs fork-join: communication is point-to-point and can happen repeatedly during execution, not only at a final join, aiming to reduce redundant exploration and lower synchronization granularity.
- Trade-off: messages are plain text (easy to integrate, model-agnostic) but consume context window and introduce robustness issues (format drift, prompt injection, context bloat).15:T6bd,The method has three layers. The first is empirical diagnosis. The authors show systematically that in ELF, more repetition often coincides with lower Gen-PPL. When repeated spans are stripped out, model rankings can reverse; the smallest model looks best largely because it repeats the most. The second layer is mechanism analysis. They rewrite self-conditioning as a feedback recurrence: the denoiser predicts a clean estimate, and that estimate becomes part of the next-step input. Linearizing this loop around sampling trajectories and inspecting the Jacobian reveals a dominant slowest-contracting mode that is nearly one-dimensional and strongly aligned with repetitive trajectories. They also run direct causal probes: increasing feedback strength alone raises repetition while lowering Gen-PPL, which supports the claim that the loop creates the pathology that the metric then rewards. The third layer is the fix, ACE (Attractor-Contrast-Escape). ACE subtracts a single fixed direction d from the self-conditioning feedback at every step. That direction is estimated without labels, auxiliary models, or retraining: collect feedback states from high-repetition trajectories and low-repetition trajectories, then take the difference of their means. The paper also derives a closed-form usable steering window so the intervention is strong enough to escape the basin but not so strong that it destroys semantics. A key design choice is where to intervene. ACE acts on the latent feedback state, not at token decoding, because the repetition is already formed upstream in continuous space before argmax readout. That makes it fundamentally different from repetition penalties, n-gram blocking, or decode-time heuristics.16:T5ab,Three result groups matter most. First is metric pathology. ELF-B reports a Gen-PPL of 19.5, but after removing repetition the score rises to 27.7, enough to change the apparent ranking against larger models. Sample-level analyses also show that more repetitive generations receive better GPT-2-based perplexity scores, confirming that the scorer favors the degenerate outputs. Second is mechanism validation. Increasing self-conditioning feedback strength increases repetition and further lowers Gen-PPL, which supports the proposed causal chain. The Jacobian analysis shows that the problem is dominated by a single slow-contracting direction, and sharper sampling settings deepen the basin. Third is ACE performance. A direction estimated once on the smallest model transfers well across inference settings and model scales; its cosine similarity to per-configuration re-estimated directions is reported between 0.82 and 0.96. Under the paper’s defect-controlled evaluation, ACE reaches human-clean text at 1.5x to 5x lower compute cost while maintaining competitive quality on accepted samples. The same direction-estimation recipe also recovers useful controls on Plaid and LangFlow, suggesting the issue is not ELF-specific but tied to self-conditioned continuous DLMs more broadly. The appendix tables on repeated 4-grams are also important: the degeneration is not mild local repetition but long loops that can persist for hundreds of words.17:T549,Read it along three threads. First, go through Section 3 and the associated tables to understand how severe the Gen-PPL failure is. Focus on how model rankings change before and after repetition stripping, and on the sample-level correlation between repetition and scorer perplexity. Second, spend real time on Section 4. This is the center of the paper: the dynamical reformulation of self-conditioning, the linearization, the Jacobian spectrum, and the feedback-strength ablations. Without that section, ACE can look like just another steering trick, which would miss the point. Third, read Section 5 carefully because the evaluation protocol is part of the contribution. The paper does not merely report lower repetition; it proposes a more defensible comparison based on a human repetition threshold, quality on the accepted set, and compute-to-clean. Figure 1, Table 4, Table 16, and Table 17 are the first items to inspect. If time is limited in the appendix, prioritize the derivation of the usable steering window and the transfer results across models and samplers. Two follow-up questions are worth keeping in mind while reading: whether the one-dimensional attractor is induced mainly by the training objective or amplified by the inference recipe, and whether training-time variants of self-conditioning could remove the issue at the source.18:T4b6,Systematic layer-wise quantization perturbation experiments first validate two core phenomena: (1) The Perplexity Illusion: PPL-sensitive layers concentrate at input embeddings and output projections, while reasoning-critical layers lie in the middle network blocks, with a Kendall τ ≈ 0 rank correlation between the two orderings. (2) The Alignment-Diversity Tradeoff: exclusive task-domain calibration produces high-variance Hessian estimates and representation collapse, while mixing in general-domain data regularizes sensitivity estimation to protect the generalization manifold, though the optimal mixing ratio varies across models and tasks with no universal heuristic. The proposed TASA framework operates on two levels: at the data level, a training-free gradient trace alignment search finds the optimal general/task calibration data ratio in |A|+1 forward passes with negligible overhead. At the network level, a Multi-Objective Aggregation metric fuses PPL and reasoning sensitivity signals; inter-layer bit allocation is formulated as a knapsack integer linear program solved exactly via dynamic programming, with intra-layer bit refinement via second-order saliency-based group assignment.19:T4c8,Anthropic frames Fable 5 and Mythos 5 as two deployments of the same base model. Fable 5 is the general-use version with strong safeguards. Mythos 5 has fewer safeguards and is limited to trusted Project Glasswing partners for defensive cybersecurity. The Fable 5 safeguard stack uses defense in depth. Some mechanisms are trained into the model so that it refuses dangerous requests. Others are external classifiers that monitor an interaction and block potentially harmful cybersecurity requests or outputs. The classifiers are intentionally set with a large safety margin: a request must look clearly safe to avoid being blocked. This reduces room for jailbreaks, but also blocks some benign coding, debugging, and defensive-security requests. After Amazon researchers reported a prompt-based bypass that made Fable 5 identify vulnerabilities and, in one case, produce exploit demonstration code, Anthropic trained a new classifier targeted at that behavior. If Fable 5 blocks a request, the user is notified and the request is routed to Opus 4.8. This is a deployment-layer mitigation rather than only a model-weight update: it can be adapted quickly to a newly observed bypass pattern, but it increases false positives.1a:T484,The post provides a small set of safety-testing results. Anthropic reports that less capable models, including Claude Opus 4.8, GPT-5.5, and Kimi K2.7, could identify the same vulnerabilities described in the Amazon report. For the exploit demonstration of the single vulnerability, every tested model could produce the same demonstration, including Claude Haiku 4.5, Sonnet 4.6, Opus 4.6, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.8, GPT-5.4, GPT-5.5, and Kimi K2.7. Anthropic therefore argues that the reported technique did not expose unique Mythos-level cyber capability, but instead hit a borderline case for Fable 5 safeguards. The new classifier blocks the specific reported technique in more than 99% of cases. In the remaining cases, Anthropic says the information is not detailed enough to be useful to an attacker. The tradeoff is a higher rate of benign requests being flagged during routine coding and debugging. The US Department of Commerce’s CAISI tested both the prior and new safeguards. The post does not provide classifier training data, benchmark size, ROC or precision-recall curves, exact false-positive rates, or the composition of the jailbreak test set.1b:T5c5,The approach is a layered runtime safety design. Fable 5 is accompanied by safety classifiers trained to distinguish multiple categories of cyber use, ordered from clearly dangerous to clearly benign. The prohibited-use category includes ransomware, wipers, cyber-physical sabotage, defense evasion, data exfiltration, malware development and delivery, offensive infrastructure, and attacks on Internet backbone systems such as BGP, DNS, certificate authorities, and NTP. These are blocked because their attacker value is high and their direct defensive value is comparatively limited. The high-risk dual-use category includes penetration testing, red teaming, exploitation, credential attacks, privilege escalation, lateral movement, persistence, exploit weaponization, security work on ICS/SCADA, telecom core, and financial infrastructure, plus high-uplift vulnerability finding. These activities may be legitimate under authorization, but prompt text alone is not a reliable authorization signal, so the system blocks them until stronger access controls are available. Lower-risk dual-use requests sit near a larger safety margin: the request must look clearly safe to pass. The classifier layer is combined with access controls, model safety training, and offline monitoring. The jailbreak framework is presented as an early shared vocabulary for rating severity by the breadth and harm of behavior unlocked, with HackerOne used as an intake channel for external findings.1c:T442,The method combines bidirectional cross-modal latent prediction with per-modality distributional regularization. Given an image encoder and a text encoder, image embeddings are passed through a modality-specific predictor to predict stop-gradient text embeddings, and text embeddings predict stop-gradient image embeddings in the reverse direction. There are no in-batch negatives, no softmax denominator, no sigmoid pair classification over all image-text pairs, no learned temperature, and no momentum teacher. Collapse is handled by SIGReg applied separately to each modality, encouraging the marginal embedding distribution to be close to an isotropic Gaussian. During training, LeVLJEPA embeddings are not L2-normalized because the regularizer operates in Euclidean space; normalization is used only at evaluation time when cosine similarity is needed. This differs from CLIP, which uses symmetric InfoNCE; from SigLIP, which uses independent pairwise sigmoid losses; and from VL-JEPA, which uses a JEPA-style prediction framing but still relies on InfoNCE negatives to avoid collapse.1d:T461,The method is evaluated at two data scales: CC12M with 12 million image-text pairs and Datacomp-L with 92 million pairs. At the CC12M scale, LeVLJEPA is reported to be competitive with CLIP and SigLIP on both zero-shot transfer and linear probing, indicating that negative-free training can learn usable cross-modal alignment at this scale. At the Datacomp-L scale, contrastive baselines retain an advantage on zero-shot transfer, which is expected because their loss directly optimizes global image-text similarity. LeVLJEPA remains on par under linear probing of the global class representation and improves background robustness. The largest separation appears in dense-token evaluations: LeVLJEPA outperforms contrastive baselines on semantic segmentation. When used as a frozen visual backbone for a VLM, with only a lightweight bridge trained between the frozen encoder and a frozen language model, it obtains the best reported accuracy on GQA, VQAv2, and POPE across two language-model families. The available text gives the training data sizes and relative outcomes, but not the full per-benchmark numeric tables.1e:T411,Read it closely. Start with the introduction to understand the shift in evaluation target: the claim is not that non-contrastive training dominates zero-shot transfer, but that it produces better dense visual features for downstream VLM use. Then read the background comparison against CLIP, SigLIP, and VL-JEPA; the key point is that VL-JEPA still uses InfoNCE and therefore remains dependent on negatives. Two follow-up questions are worth investigating: how SIGReg interacts with embedding dimension, batch size, and data scale; and whether this objective can be combined with autoregressive multimodal pretraining or diffusion-style visual objectives.1f:T8ca,The proposed method is Asymmetric Mutual Variational Learning (AMVL). The key idea is not just to add two KL terms, but to treat the prior and posterior as fundamentally different objects with different roles. The posterior q(z|x,y) is answer-aware and used during training to discover useful latent reasoning trajectories. The prior p(z|x) is answer-agnostic and must generate usable latent states at inference time. Because their conditioning structures differ, a one-sided ELBO is insufficient.

The model introduces a sequence of continuous latent variables Z = [z1, ..., zk] as intermediate reasoning states between multimodal input x and answer y. The generative model factorizes into p_theta(Z|x), the inference-time prior, and p_theta(y|x,Z), an autoregressive decoder conditioned on both the input and the latent sequence. Training uses a target-aware posterior to approximate the intractable true posterior over latent states.

AMVL then applies two complementary regularizers:
1. a forward KL term that aligns the prior toward posterior-inferred latent states, so the inference-time prior does not drift away from the useful latent space discovered during training;
2. a reverse KL term that constrains the posterior relative to the learned prior, preventing it from moving too far into answer-dependent shortcut regions outside the prior’s support.

This is why the method is called asymmetric mutual learning. The two distributions are not symmetric peers. One is answer-aware, the other is not, and they serve different downstream roles. Compared with standard ELBO training, the difference is that the posterior is no longer treated as an unrestricted teacher. Compared with prior latent reasoning work, the difference is that AMVL does not rely on external reconstruction or alignment losses to define what the latent states should contain. Instead, the answer-driven objective shapes the latent space, while reverse KL keeps that space inference-compatible.

Architecturally, the implementation appears lightweight: a latent-integrated MLLM with variational heads rather than a major backbone redesign. So the main contribution is the training objective and the probabilistic framing, not a new large-scale multimodal architecture.20:T5fc,From the available full text, the experimental claim is that AMVL consistently outperforms both discrete reasoning baselines and prior latent-reasoning methods on challenging multimodal benchmarks. However, the visible content does not include the full benchmark list, model sizes, latent lengths, or the detailed result tables, so it is not possible to reproduce all quantitative details with confidence.

Even so, the intended experimental structure is clear. The paper appears to compare against three categories:
1. discrete text-based reasoning baselines, to test whether continuous latent reasoning alleviates the language-space bottleneck;
2. existing latent reasoning methods, to test whether AMVL’s self-contained variational learning is better than hand-crafted latent supervision;
3. ablations removing either the forward KL or the reverse KL, to test whether one-sided training is the source of the mismatch and whether both calibration terms matter.

The most important empirical point is likely not the headline score but the evidence that one-way posterior-to-prior matching is insufficient. If adding reverse KL improves inference-time prior quality, that supports the paper’s central diagnosis: posterior shortcutting harms generalization because the prior cannot faithfully reproduce answer-conditioned latent structure at test time. Any reported latent visualizations, prior-posterior gap statistics, or KL-weight ablations would be especially important for judging whether the method is doing what it claims.21:T4c5,This is better suited for a skim than a deep read unless you are actively working on latent reasoning. Focus on the problem setup and the objective, not the task-specific multimodal details. Start with the introduction to understand that the target is not merely the weakness of discrete CoT, but the mismatch created by answer-conditioned posterior training. Then go straight to the method section and read the conditional generative formulation, the ELBO discussion, and the two KL terms in AMVL. The key question is who each term constrains and why a one-sided KL is not enough.

If you want to adapt the idea to language-only latent reasoning, then it is worth reading more carefully for posterior parameterization, how latent states are injected into the decoder, and any training-stability details.22:T411,SpiralFovea is a parameter-free input-adaptive tokenizer. It converts the image to luminance, downsamples and quantizes it, then computes local Shannon entropy with a sliding window. The image is split into horizontal strips; within each active strip, the highest-entropy location is selected as an anchor, followed by radius-based deduplication to avoid collapsing all anchors onto one hotspot. Around each retained anchor, the method extracts multi-scale spiral rings: a central foveal patch plus three outer rings with increasing radius and patch size. For 224-pixel images, the schedule uses [(24,0),(28,18),(36,22),(48,26)], radii [0,26,58,96], and up to 29 patches per anchor. Out-of-bounds patches are filtered, leading to at most about 78 retained patches for most samples. The key difference from token pruning is timing: SpiralFovea decides which tokens exist before patch projection and before self-attention, whereas pruning usually forms the full grid first and removes tokens only after some computation has already been spent.23:T720,The key idea is Anchored Stochastic Flow Map Composition, or ASFMC. The paper first makes a negative point: for velocity fields or finely discretized ODE samplers, one can introduce stochasticity through a path-preserving SDE reformulation. For long-range flow maps, however, the stochastic transition from source time t to target time r generally depends on the entire intermediate stochastic path. It cannot, in general, be reduced to a simple Gaussian perturbation around the deterministic flow-map output. Exact SDE stochasticization would therefore require stochastic integration and would largely destroy the efficiency advantage of few-step generators.

ASFMC avoids direct stochasticization of the long-range map. Instead, it introduces an anchor time τ and constructs a stochastic transition by composition. Starting from a deterministic transition t to r, the method first maps the sample to the deterministic endpoint at time r using the pretrained flow map. It then transports that endpoint to an anchor time τ and resamples back to time r through a conditional transition defined on the probability path. This yields a stochastic kernel for the t to r step while preserving the marginal path learned by the original model.

The framework is instantiated for both major few-step flow-map parameterizations: single-time endpoint maps such as sCM, and two-time maps such as MeanFlow. For the latter, the paper discusses local and endpoint anchors. Once ASFMC is in place, each sampling step becomes a stochastic policy transition, so the few-step generator can be cast as an MDP and optimized with GRPO-style likelihood ratios. A major practical point is that this is post hoc: the pretrained flow-map parameterization is kept intact, and the method does not fall back to expensive fine-step ODE or SDE sampling.24:T577,The experiments are conducted on few-step FLUX-based text-to-image generators, with pretrained MeanFlow and sCM checkpoints as the starting point. The paper reports improvements after RL post-training on reward-based, perceptual, and task-level metrics. That suggests ASFMC is not only a formal device for making the RL objective well-defined, but also useful in practice for aligning image generators to downstream rewards.

From the available text, the experimental agenda appears to have three parts. First, show that Flow-Map GRPO improves over the pretrained checkpoints under reward optimization. Second, compare ASFMC against simpler stochasticization baselines or unsuitable SDE approximations to demonstrate better stability and better preservation of the original probability path. Third, validate the method on both sCM and MeanFlow to support the claim of parameterization-agnostic applicability.

The provided excerpt does not include the full tables, so I cannot safely state exact step counts, reward gains, FID changes, or preference improvements. When reading the paper, the numbers to verify are: how many sampling steps are used, how anchor choice affects variance and performance, whether RL post-training degrades perceptual quality, and how stable the likelihood-ratio estimates are. Those details determine whether the method is mainly elegant on paper or genuinely practical.25:T4af,Read this one with a narrow focus. Start with the section that argues why SDE-based stochasticization does not directly apply to long-range flow maps. That negative result is the foundation of the paper. If that argument is weak, the motivation for ASFMC becomes much less compelling.

Then study the ASFMC construction carefully. The role of the anchor time τ is the conceptual center: it is not just an auxiliary variable, but the mechanism that connects a deterministic endpoint map to a path-preserving stochastic conditional transition. It is worth checking the derivation to see exactly why the marginal probability path is preserved.

Next, compare the two instantiations for sCM and MeanFlow. Many papers claim a unified framework but become awkward when moved across parameterizations. Here, the important question is whether the transition kernel and likelihood-ratio formulation remain clean in both cases.

Finally, inspect the ablations on anchor choice, number of steps, reward improvement, and image-quality trade-offs. For triage, about 30 minutes is enough. If you work on diffusion or flow-model post-training, the method derivation and experimental details are worth a full read.26:T9b0,The methodology is simple but well controlled. The authors define a metric called layer contribution: train only layer l with RL while freezing all other layers, then measure what fraction of the improvement from full-parameter RL is recovered relative to the same base model. This normalized recovery ratio makes comparisons possible across models and tasks.

The paper then builds around three experiment families.

First, single-layer isolation. Each transformer layer is trained independently under RL. The best single layer can recover up to 114% of the gain from full-parameter RL, while weak layers recover less than 30%. So the answer to “is one layer enough?” is not uniformly yes; it depends strongly on which layer is chosen.

Second, stability analysis. The authors repeat the measurement across seven models from two families (Qwen3 and Qwen2.5), three RL algorithms (GRPO, GiGPO, Dr. GRPO), and three domains: mathematical reasoning, code generation, and agentic decision-making. High-contribution layers consistently cluster in the middle of the stack. For a fixed model, per-layer rankings are strongly correlated across datasets and even across tasks: Spearman rho is 0.76 between NuminaMath-CoT and DeepScaleR, and 0.59 between NuminaMath-CoT and DeepCoder. This suggests the pattern is not a dataset artifact but a stable property of how RL exploits pretrained depth structure.

Third, algorithmic exploitation. Guided by the measured contributions, the authors propose layer-aware RL that prioritizes high-contribution layers instead of updating all layers uniformly. On Qwen3-8B, training only the ten highest-contribution layers reaches 69.1% average accuracy on math benchmarks, compared with 66.4% for full-parameter RL. They also show that models trained on different layers exhibit complementary behaviors, and majority voting among them yields further gains.

Mechanistically, the paper points toward a plausible interpretation: lower layers are tied more to lexical and local representation building, upper layers are closer to output formatting and surface realization, while middle layers are where RL can most effectively reshape reasoning transformations and decision boundaries. The paper does not fully prove this mechanism, but the empirical case is coherent. Unlike typical PEFT work, the contribution here is not to assume a compact update form first, but to measure where adaptation actually matters and then design around that structure.27:T639,The empirical coverage is fairly broad. The study spans two model families (Qwen3 and Qwen2.5), seven models in total, three RL algorithms (GRPO, GiGPO, Dr. GRPO), and three task domains: math reasoning, code generation, and agentic decision-making. The most important numbers are:

1. Best single-layer recovery: up to 114% of the gain from full-parameter RL, while the weakest layers recover under 30%.
2. Ranking stability: Spearman rho = 0.76 for per-layer contribution rankings between NuminaMath-CoT and DeepScaleR, and rho = 0.59 between NuminaMath-CoT and DeepCoder.
3. Layer-aware training result: on Qwen3-8B, training only the top-10 layers by contribution yields 69.1% average accuracy on math benchmarks versus 66.4% for full-parameter RL.

The most important ablation insight is that the pattern is structured rather than noisy. If layer contribution were just optimization variance, rankings would not remain stable across algorithms, datasets, and tasks. Another notable result is complementarity: models trained on different layers make different mistakes, and ensembling them through majority voting improves beyond the full-parameter baseline. That hints that layer-wise RL may explore distinct strategy subspaces that joint full-model training partially collapses.

From a pretraining-oriented perspective, it is also important that the comparisons are made under controlled settings. The paper frames layer contribution under iso-settings, which matters because otherwise single-layer training could look good merely due to easier optimization rather than higher leverage.28:T50a,This is best read for experimental design rather than mathematical novelty. Start with the introduction to understand the framing, then go directly to the section defining layer contribution so the comparison protocol is clear. After that, focus on three sets of figures and tables:

1. The per-layer contribution curves. These show whether the middle-layer peak is sharp and whether the shape is consistent across models.
2. The cross-dataset and cross-task rank-correlation results. These are the backbone of the “stable structure” claim.
3. The layer-aware RL results versus full-parameter RL, plus the ensemble or majority-voting analysis. This is where the paper moves from diagnosis to actionable method.

Two questions are worth keeping in mind while reading. Why are middle layers the main carriers of RL gains rather than top layers? And is this phenomenon specific to RL, or would strong supervised post-training show a similar depth profile? If the latter, the paper is more about a general adaptation law; if the former, it says something specific about how reward signals couple to pretrained representations. Around 30 to 45 minutes should be enough to extract the main ideas, with appendix details worth checking if you care about robustness across models and algorithms.29:T4b6,They run repeated Lewis signaling games:
- Sender observes 4 candidate objects and a designated target; Receiver observes the same 4 candidates plus the message, then selects the target.
- Object space has 8 structured referents (color × shape × size). Messages have fixed length L over a constrained vocabulary V; channel capacity is varied by sweeping |V| and L, with cap ranging from 4 to 125.
- Main intervention: five memory architectures (the excerpt confirms at least stateless/rolling-context variants and a persistent private notebook). The notebook externalizes learned conventions so agents do not need to re-derive the code each round.
- Three studies: (1) compare memory architectures at a fixed channel; (2) sweep capacity to obtain capacity–performance curves per memory design; (3) disentangle consolidation effects from mere history length, testing whether notebooks help because they are persistent/structured rather than simply longer context.
- Conceptual contrast: classic information-bottleneck arguments suggest cap=8 (matching the number of objects) might favor compositionality; they find it is instead a fragility point for LLM agents, with success dominated by memory design.2a:T448,This is not a pretraining paper in the narrow sense, so its value for foundation-model researchers is indirect. The main contribution is a concrete design pattern for turning language models into more reliable open-ended discovery agents: persistent memory, executable hypotheses, external statistical validation, and reflection over accumulated claims. Three aspects matter. First, it separates propose, evaluate, and reflect as distinct functions rather than treating the whole loop as prompt chaining. Second, it insists that discoveries must be grounded in executable code and statistical tests, which is a useful reminder that in open-ended research settings the LLM should generate candidate analyses, not serve as the evidence source. Third, the new benchmark setup, especially iNatDisco, is relevant for evaluating whether a system is actually exploring and rediscovering latent structure rather than merely paraphrasing familiar patterns. For a pretraining-oriented reader, this is better viewed as an agentic discovery paper with implications for memory, tool use, and test-time search.2b:T73b,The central move is to formalize open-ended discovery as a stateful Propose-Evaluate-Reflect loop. A hypothesis is not a text statement but an executable program over the dataset X. The program returns a binary judgment, supported or rejected, together with statistical evidence. Only hypotheses that pass significance and effect-size thresholds are promoted into a persistent claim store C_t. Rejected hypotheses are also retained. This design constrains the LLM to hypothesis generation and analysis scripting, while outsourcing factual adjudication to statistical procedures.

The main novelty is the Reflect stage. Instead of simply appending prior findings into the next prompt, DiscoPER periodically analyzes the accepted and rejected claim sets to produce structured guidance G_t. The paper highlights two kinds of reflective signals: under-explored variables and recurring confounds across multiple discovered relationships. That guidance then conditions the next round of hypothesis generation. In scientific terms, the system tries to do two things humans often do: fill obvious gaps in coverage, and infer higher-order structure from repeated local findings.

The framework is also multimodal and code-based. Compared with classical structure-learning methods, it supports a much broader hypothesis space than pairwise relations or fixed graph templates. Compared with AI-scientist systems that require seed questions, it aims for question-free exploration. Compared with generic code agents, it adds persistent claim accumulation and explicit meta-reflection. The use of held-out validation is also important: discoveries are not accepted solely because they fit the same data used to search for them. That does not fully solve multiple-testing issues, but it is a more defensible setup than unconstrained pattern mining on a single split.2c:T5d4,The headline result is that DiscoPER outperforms baselines on multiple discovery benchmarks, and that the reflection mechanism improves exploration quality. On the newly introduced iNatDisco benchmark, the system reportedly rediscovers more than 60% of real-world expert-verified patterns. That is the most important number in the paper because it directly measures whether the system can recover meaningful latent regularities in an open-ended setting.

From the available full-text sections, several experimental choices are clear. First, the benchmarks are constructed from real scientific data with literature-grounded expert claims, rather than from fixed question-answer tasks. Second, outputs are filtered through significance and effect-size criteria, so evaluation is tied to data-backed findings rather than fluent textual hypotheses. Third, the paper includes comparisons with and without reflection, which is critical for testing whether meta-level guidance adds value beyond simple claim memory.

The provided text does not include the full result tables, so I cannot safely state all baseline names, exact metric values, iteration counts, or dataset sizes. When reading the paper, the numbers worth checking carefully are: the scale of iNatDisco, the gain from reflection over a no-reflection variant, and the precision or rediscovery rate with and without held-out validation. Those details determine whether the contribution is mainly conceptual or already robust in practice.2d:T55e,This is a skim paper for a pretraining-focused reader. Four parts deserve attention.

First, read the introduction and the framework summary table. The paper’s case rests on three requirements for open-ended discovery: no predefined question, an unrestricted executable hypothesis space, and reflective search conditioned on prior findings. If those requirements do not hold, the rest of the system matters less.

Second, read the method section around the formal definitions of hypothesis, claim, guidance, and claim store. The real contribution is not that the model writes code, but that prior accepted and rejected findings are converted into structured guidance that changes the future search distribution.

Third, inspect the benchmark construction, especially iNatDisco. Check how expert-verified claims are sourced, whether there is leakage from public knowledge, and how multimodal inputs such as images and metadata are used.

Fourth, focus on the ablations. Reflection, held-out validation, statistical thresholds, and persistent memory are the components that need to justify themselves. If those ablations are weak, the work is closer to system assembly than a durable methodological step.

For triage, 20 to 30 minutes is enough. For anyone working on research agents or autonomous experimentation, the method and evaluation sections are worth a closer read.2e:T991,方法核心是一个四层架构。L0 是冻结的基础模型；L1 是小型 steering adapter，用来在线调整策略但不在文中实验里做常规权重微调；L2 是可变、可版本化的 harness，包括提示、工具、执行环境包装、测试脚本等；L3 是 loop controller，负责决定何时接受一次修改。作者的关键约束是：所有修改都必须经过 anytime-valid gate。这里的 gate 不是普通 early stopping，而是基于 anytime-valid inference、e-process、confidence sequence、spending schedule 等序贯统计工具，在固定错误预算下决定是否接受一个候选修改，并输出可审计证书。

围绕内生闭环的五类失败模式，作者设计了五个控制器：稳定性-可塑性、自指坍塌、信用分配、可验证自修改、假设空间扩张。论文的写法比较克制，明确说这些控制器调用的是已有理论结果，自己没有证明这些保证在内生闭环下仍然成立，只是把“经典保证在什么条件下还能部分存活”工程化地包起来。这个态度是加分项。

另一个重要部分是 verifier-in-the-loop。作者认为 gate 只能在“基础模型原本就会、但出现概率低”的行为中做选择；如果基础模型系统性不会，单靠 gate 无法创造能力。因此他们把 verifier 变成密集控制信号，而不是只在 episode 末端给一个成败标签。具体机制包括 best-of-N、micro-step search、过程级奖励、search-layer control、自写 reproduction oracle，以及 harness self-repair。尤其是 self-authored oracle：模型先根据 issue 自己写一组 reproduction tests，只要这些测试在未修补基线下失败，就允许其进入搜索环；真正 held-out grader 只用于最终测量，不参与中间 steering。这个设计试图减少 grader 泄漏，同时给搜索提供更稠密的反馈。

论文还提出 search-then-distill 的双环结构：外环在搜索层上做受控探索，内环再把搜索得到的行为蒸馏回较稳定的策略层。五个控制器里有四个被重新安置到 search layer 上，并通过离线 gate simulation 做验证。统计工具箱部分很全，包括 normal-mixture confidence sequences、Hoeffding e-process、coin-betting、Wasserstein drift 检测、wild bootstrap trend test、MAP-Elites archive、MDL compression 等，但更像一个可复用控制库，而不是单一算法创新。2f:T9de,The system is organized into four layers. L0 is a frozen pretrained model. L1 is a small steering adapter that changes policy online; in the reported setup it is not standard weight fine-tuning in the usual sense, but a constrained steering mechanism. L2 is a mutable, versioned harness containing prompts, tools, wrappers, execution logic, and tests. L3 is a loop controller that decides whether a proposed modification should be accepted.

The central mechanism is the anytime-valid gate. Every self-modification must pass through a sequential statistical test that spends from a fixed error budget and emits an auditable certificate. The paper draws on existing tools such as anytime-valid inference, e-processes, confidence sequences, harmonic spending schedules, and related sequential testing machinery. Importantly, the authors are careful about epistemic status: they do not claim new proofs for endogenous closed loops. Instead, they package existing guarantees and state explicitly where survival of those guarantees remains conjectural once the environment becomes policy-dependent.

The paper then maps five endogenous-loop failure modes to five controllers: stability-plasticity, self-referential collapse, credit assignment, verifiable self-modification, and hypothesis-space expansion. The design choice that matters most is the restriction that gates can only select among behaviors already present in the frozen base model. That means a gate cannot create capability where the base model fails systematically. To address this, the verifier is moved into the loop as an active control signal. The mechanisms include best-of-N, micro-step search, process-level reward, search-layer control, self-authored reproduction oracles, and harness self-repair.

The self-authored oracle mechanism is one of the more interesting pieces. Given an issue, the model writes its own reproduction tests. An oracle suite is admitted if it fails on the unpatched base system. Search can then proceed using these in-loop tests, while the held-out grader is reserved for final measurement only. The paper also proposes a search-then-distill two-loop design: controlled exploration happens in the search layer, and successful behavior is later distilled into a more stable policy layer. A large statistical toolbox supports the framework, including normal-mixture confidence sequences, Hoeffding e-processes, coin-betting oracles, Wasserstein drift checks, wild-bootstrap trend tests, MAP-Elites archives, and MDL-style compression.30:T55f,Read it in the order of problem setup, architectural constraints, controller design, and statistical gating. Start with the introduction to understand the endogenous-loop failure mode and why the paper insists on a frozen L0, a low-dimensional L1, and a mutable external harness. Then read the four-layer architecture section carefully, because the entire framework depends on what is allowed to change and what must remain fixed. In the controller section, focus less on the formalism and more on the mapping from each failure mode to a concrete control mechanism and to the specific prior theorem it borrows from. The most useful section is the verifier-in-the-loop part, especially the self-authored reproduction oracle, verified micro-step search, and the search-then-distill two-loop design. The statistical core is best treated as a reference section unless you are actively working on sequential testing or safe online adaptation.

If time is limited, inspect Figure 1, Table 2, and Algorithms 6-10 first, then return to the introduction and architecture section. The main questions worth probing are whether the statistical assumptions behind the gates survive real agent environments, whether self-authored oracles create new reward-hacking channels, and whether restricting updates to behaviors already latent in the base model makes the framework too conservative.3:["$","main",null,{"className":"flex-1 overflow-y-auto","children":["$","div",null,{"className":"mx-auto max-w-4xl px-4 py-8 sm:px-6 xl:max-w-6xl","children":["$","$L9",null,{"data":{"date":"2026-07-03","generated_at":"2026-07-03T00:42:50.386030+00:00","stats":{"total":98,"starred":6,"must_read":9,"skim":32,"glance":57,"by_category":{"pretraining_data":3,"industry":2,"llm_pretrain":2,"scaling":3,"architecture":9,"efficient":5,"eval":15,"multimodal":13,"multimodal_gen":6,"post_train":8,"mechanism":13,"coding_agent":4,"agent":15},"trending_repos":10,"trending_hf":10,"fetch_status":{"arxiv":"ok","huggingface":"empty","blog":"ok","twitter":"empty","github_trending":"ok","hf_trending":"ok"},"fetch_errors":{},"last_fetch_attempt":1783043154.6771631},"categories":[{"id":"pretraining_data","emoji":"📚","name":"预训练数据","description":"以预训练数据为核心贡献的工作：数据采集/清洗/去重、数据配比(data mixture)、数据筛选/质量评估、语料构建、数据课程(data curriculum)、数据重复(repetition)、合成数据用于预训练、Tokenizer/词表设计、成员推断/数据检测。判据是『论文核心是数据本身』，而非训练 recipe/优化器(→ llm_pretrain)或评测资产(→ eval)","papers":[{"id":"2607.00890","title":"MultiSynt/MT: Trillion-Token Multi-Parallel Pre-Training Data Translated Across 36 Languages","abstract":"Open web-scale pre-training corpora remain concentrated in English, limiting multilingual LLM development. European languages, openly available native pretraining corpora are often too small or too noisy to support strong LLM training. The authors take a synthetic-data route at unusual scale. They translate 100B high-quality English tokens into 36 languages, producing MultiSynt/MT, an open corpus with about 4.8T target-language tokens. The real question is not merely whether large translated corpora can be built, but how far translated pretraining data can substitute for native corpora, where it helps, where it fails because of translationese and English-centered cultural grounding, and whether current multilingual benchmarks can even detect those differences.","importance":"Its value for pretraining research is threefold. First, it turns machine-translated multilingual pretraining from a small-scale idea into a trillion-token open resource. That is immediately useful for languages with weak native data availability. Second, it provides a concrete data-efficiency result: on standard multilingual base-model benchmarks, models trained on MultiSynt/MT can match a native-data baseline with roughly 72% fewer pretraining tokens, and at a matched 100B-token budget they improve by about 15% relative. That suggests high-quality translated text can be a denser training signal than existing open native corpora, not just a weaker fallback. Third, the paper is careful about the limits. 结合。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a practical failure mode in data-mixture optimization for LLM training, especially in SFT: existing methods usually learn a global mapping from mixture weights to downstream performance from historical proxy runs, implicitly assuming that the optimal mixture is stable across data pools. The authors argue that this is false. Once the underlying pool changes in quality, difficulty, style, or complexity, the marginal value of each domain can change substantially, including sign flips from positive transfer to negative transfer. The real problem, then, is not to memorize one best recipe, but to estimate how the return of each domain depends on the current state of the data pool.","importance":"This is a strong fit for pretraining-adjacent research because it reframes mixture design as a transferable estimation problem rather than a one-off hyperparameter search. The contribution matters in three ways. First, it separates data-state effects from mixture effects, which is exactly the confounding that often makes mixture heuristics brittle in pretraining, continual pretraining, and curriculum design. Second, it aims to learn state-conditioned marginal returns that can transfer to unseen pools and larger models, which is much closer to a scalable recipe-learning setup than static proxy regression. Third, the method produces interpretable conditional treatment effects, and the paper uses them to surface concrete phenomena such as conflicts between factual knowledge and complex reasoning, and quality thresholds for math and code data. In this work, we study the effect of such staleness in asynchronous GRPO. We make the behavior policy explicit in the GRPO surrogate objective and distinguish between the surrogate-gradient mapping used by the learner and the true total derivative of a distribution-dependent population objective. Under assumptions of local boundedness, distribution","starred":true,"priority":"p1","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-01T15:40:12Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2607_01083.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"大规模异步RLHF流水线（尤其是当前推理增强对齐主流采用的GRPO方案，如DeepSeek-R1所用架构）中，rollout生成与策略优化解耦会产生陈旧rollout问题——即生成经验的行为策略滞后于学习器当前策略。现有异步SGD分析仅将滞后程度（staleness）视为有界延迟，未量化其与策略梯度固有的分布偏移、学习率之间的交互作用，也无法解释异步GRPO中普遍观测到的「先稳定提升后突然崩溃」的两阶段训练动态，更缺乏可落地的学习率与滞后程度匹配规则。","importance":"该工作填补了现有分布式RL理论与主流GRPO-based RLHF实践之间的空白，针对无 critic、无显式off-policy校正、仅依赖重要性采样比裁剪与KL正则的现代RLHF场景，建立了滞后程度-学习率缩放律的理论框架，给出了稳定训练的闭式约束条件。结论可直接指导工业级大规模异步RLHF的系统设计与超参调优，减少试错成本、避免训练崩溃，属于预训练-后训练闭环中对齐优化环节的基础理论+可落地规则类贡献，可直接影响工业级系统的配置策略。","technical_approach":"核心技术方案分为四个部分，与现有方法的核心区别在于完全适配GRPO的无 critic 结构与当前RLHF的无显式off-policy校正设定：1. 行为策略感知的GRPO代理目标重构：不同于默认行为策略与当前学习策略等价的传统GRPO推导，显式将生成rollout的陈旧行为策略φ与学习器参数θ解耦，定义代理梯度映射ℋ(θ,φ)，避免将代理梯度与分布依赖的总体目标全导数混为一谈，为滞后偏差分析提供了严谨基础。2. 单步滞后偏差量化：在局部有界、分布平滑、行为策略平滑三个假设下，严格证明陈旧rollout引入的单步代理梯度偏差量级为O(Sη)，其中S为最大rollout滞后步数、η为学习率，即单步训练的安全区域由Sη的乘积控制。3. 条件崩溃时间缩放律：区分两类约束——GRPO重要性采样比的batch级裁剪半径、训练时域内的学习器漂移阈值，将训练稳定性分为两种状态区间（regime）：当Sη足够小、单步滞后偏差未超出batch裁剪容忍范围时，崩溃时间遵循Tη（T为训练步数）的时域律，此时最大稳定学习率几乎与S无关；当Sη超出batch裁剪容忍范围时，稳定性直接由Sη约束。4. 双约束稳定规则：结合两种状态区间给出可直接落地的超参配置公式η ≪ min{R_batch/(S·G_upd), R_crit/(T·G_upd)}，其中R_batch为batch级裁剪半径、R_crit为临界漂移阈值、G_upd为单步梯度幅度，解释了工业实践中有时观察到的「最大稳定学习率与滞后程度无关」的现象仅出现在时域约束主导的场景。","experiments":"当前公开的全文片段未包含完整实验章节，摘要及理论推导部分未给出具体实验数值（如参数量、数据规模、性能提升幅度）；所有结论均基于三个明确假设的解析推导，给出了偏差量级与稳定约束的闭式表达。","reading_guide":"精读优先级如下：1. 引言部分：重点理解异步GRPO的吞吐动机、现有分析的核心缺陷，以及「先稳后崩」两阶段训练动态的观测背景。2. 行为策略感知的GRPO代理重构章节：重点理解ℋ(θ,φ)的定义逻辑，明确传统GRPO推导无法处理滞后问题的核心原因。3. 单步滞后偏差证明：关注三个假设的适用边界——例如当策略更新过于激进时，局部有界假设会失效，此时结论不再成立。4. 条件崩溃时间缩放律推导：重点理解两种状态区间的切换条件，即batch级裁剪约束与时域漂移约束的权衡逻辑。5. scalabili","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-01T15:35:04Z","category_id":"scaling","cover_url":"/covers/2607_01077.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"在推理时扩展（inference-time Passing Language Models (MPLMs)：\n- 把一次推理拆成多个并发“线程”（每个线程有独立 prompt/context 和 ID），并通过 batched inference 并行解码。\n- 让模型在输出中生成带标签的控制指令字符串，由外部控制器解释执行，形成类似 MPI 的原语：\n  1）spawn：创建若干新线程，并把给定 prompt 拼接进各自上下文；\n  2）send：向指定线程集合发送一段文本消息；\n  3）recv：阻塞等待来自指定线程集合的消息（同步点）；\n  4）stop：终止线程。\n- 推理引擎层面：不改 token sampling 与 KV-cache 管理，只在其上加一个轻量 controller，负责线程生命周期、消息队列、阻塞/唤醒、以及把“收到的消息”注入线程上下文。\n- 与传统 fork-join 的关键差异：FJ 通常只在 join 时汇总，线程间缺少点对点、过程中的信息交换；MPLM 允许在推理过程中多次 send/recv，减少重复探索与无效分支，并把同步粒度从“阶段级”降到“消息级”。\n- 取舍：通信载体是文本消息（进入上下文），实现简单、可复用现有 LLM；但也意味着消息会占用上下文窗口，并引入提示注入/格式鲁棒性问题。论文的抽象更偏“可执行提示语言 + 调度器”，而不是训练一个原生并行架构。","experiments":"给出的全文片段主要覆盖框架与实现逻辑（preliminaries、指令设计、推理控制层），未包含完整实验表格与具体数值细节（例如在何种推理基准上、相对 CoT/FJ 的 token on parallel fork-join (FJ) patterns, but FJ threads are typically ephemeral and only merge at coarse join points. This limits scalability due to duplicated work and high coordination overhead. The paper targets a practical question: how to enable fine-grained, point-to-point communication among concurrent LLM “threads” while reusing existing batched inference engines and KV-cache implementations, so that parallel reasoning becomes more efficient than long sequential CoT.","importance":"This is not a pretraining paper (no new objective/data/optimizer), but it matters as an interface between inference-time reasoning and future training recipes: (1) it turns multi-thread reasoning into an explicit, learnable control language (spawn/send/recv/stop), which is a clean substrate for trajectory synthesis and mid-training; (2) it reframes scaling limits around communication/state management rather than raw compute, hinting that pretraining for native parallel coordination may require explicit modeling of thread state and messages; (3) it provides an implementable systems abstraction on top of vLLM/SGLang-style batched serving, which is relevant if you care about how agentic traces are actually produced at scale.","technical_approach":"$14","experiments":"The provided full-text excerpt mainly covers the framework design and runtime logic; it does not include the main experimental tables or concrete numbers (e.g., token savings, wall-clock speedups, accuracy gains vs CoT/FJ, scaling with number of threads, or ablations on send/recv). By default these attempts are generated independently, wasting inference compute on redundant solutions. This waste seems unavoidable. After all, independence is what makes parallel sampling trivial to scale. However, this tradeoff is not fundamental: there is a rich design space of samplers that generate correlated but exact samples entirely in p","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-01T17:10:08Z","category_id":"scaling","cover_url":"/covers/2607_01179.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文处理的是 test-time scaling 和 RL 训练里的采样冗余问题：同一个 prompt 下并行生成 k 个答案时，i.i.d. 采样常常把计算浪费在相近的高概率区域，导致 pass@k 提升不够高效，也会让 GRPO 这类组内采样方法需要更大的 group size 才能得到足够的 reward variation。","importance":"对预训练研究的直接贡献不在训练新基座，而在“采样分布的联合设计”这个接口上。它提醒一个关键点：很多 LM 估计器只要求每条 rollout 的边缘分布正确，不要求 k 条样本相互独立。只要保持每个样本仍然是模型分布下的 exact sample，就可以通过相关采样提升覆盖率，同时避免改 logits、改 prompt 或顺序条件采样带来的偏差和并行性损失。这个思想对 test-time compute、RLVR/GRPO 数据生成、best-of-N、pass@k 评估都有参考价值。","technical_approach":"QuasiMoTTo 用 randomized Quasi-Monte Carlo 先生成一组在 [0,1] 上分布更均匀、彼此带排斥性的随机数，再通过 arithmetic coding / inverse-CDF sampling 把这些随机数映射成语言模型序列。关键设计是：每个随机数的边缘分布仍然是 uniform，因此映射后的每条序列边缘上仍然严格服从原 LM；但这些随机数的联合分布不是独立的，所以 k 条生成之间会减少重复覆盖。与顺序去重或 diversity-promoting decoding 不同，它不需要样本之间通信，也不改变模型的 next-token 分布。与温度、top-p、prompt perturbation 这类方法不同，它追求的是 marginal-preserving 的覆盖改善，而不是用启发式方式改变采样分布。","experiments":"实验关注两个场景。第一是 reasoning 任务上的 test-time scaling，用 pass@k 衡量固定样本数下的覆盖收益，并与一个 union-bound ceiling 比较。论文报告 QuasiMoTTo 在 reasoning benchmarks 上接近这个 marginal-preserving sampler 能达到的上界，说明主要收益来自减少样本之间的重复，而不是改变单样本质量。第二是 RL 场景，将 QuasiMoTTo 作为 i.i.d. sampling 的替换项接入类似 GRPO 的训练流程。结果显示，在达到同等目标性能时，所需训练步数少于 i.i.d. 采样。给出的全文片段没有包含具体表格数值、模型规模和每个 benchmark 的逐项增益，因此不应把“接近上界”和“减少步数”解读为固定倍率结论。","reading_guide":"泛读建议用 20–30 分钟。先看方法部分中 marginal correctness 的推导，重点是为什么 average-type estimator 只需要正确边缘分布。再看 arithmetic sampling 如何把一个 uniform 随机变量映射为完整 autoregressive 序列，这是并行 exact sampling 的核心。实验部分建议重点看 pass@k 与 union-bound ceiling 的图，以及 RL 训练曲线。需要留意一个细节：如果 RL estimator 使用 group-dependent baseline 或 ranking normalization，相关采样对方差和偏差的影响要比无 baseline 的平均估计更细，需要看论文如何处理。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper targets redundancy in parallel sampling for test-time scaling and RL. When k completions are drawn independently for the same prompt, many attempts fall into the same high-probability regions. This wastes inference compute for pass@k-style reasoning and also weakens within-group reward variation in methods such as GRPO.","importance":"The main value for foundation-model research is the sampling interface, not a new pretraining recipe. The paper makes a useful distinction: many estimators need each rollout to have the correct marginal distribution, but they do not require the k rollouts to be independent. This leaves room to design the joint distribution of a batch so that samples cover the model distribution better while remaining exact samples from the original LM. The idea is relevant to test-time compute, RLVR data generation, best-of-N procedures, pass@k evaluation, and group-based policy optimization.","technical_approach":"QuasiMoTTo first uses randomized Quasi-Monte Carlo to produce k points in the unit interval that are more evenly spread than i.i.d. uniforms. It then maps each point to a full autoregressive LM sample through arithmetic coding, equivalently an inverse-CDF view of sequence sampling. Each point is marginally uniform, so each decoded sequence is marginally an exact sample from the LM. The samples are not independent, which creates a repulsive structure across the batch and reduces duplicated coverage. Unlike sequential conditioning, the rollouts can be decoded in parallel. Unlike temperature changes, top-p changes, prompt perturbations, or diversity-biased decoding, the method does not alter the model’s per-sample distribution.","experiments":"The experiments are organized around two uses. For test-time scaling, the paper compares pass@k on reasoning benchmarks and also reports a union-bound ceiling for what any marginal-preserving sampler could achieve. QuasiMoTTo is reported to come close to that ceiling, suggesting that much of the available gain comes from reducing overlap among samples. For RL, it replaces i.i.d. sampling inside a GRPO-like pipeline and reaches target performance in fewer training steps. The provided full-text excerpt does not include exact per-benchmark numbers, model sizes, or step-reduction ratios, so the result should be read as a qualitative efficiency gain unless the tables are inspected directly.","reading_guide":"A 20–30 minute skim is sufficient. Focus first on the marginal-correctness argument: average estimators only need correct rollout marginals, not independent rollouts. In the experiments, inspect the pass@k versus union-bound plots and the RL learning curves. t","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-01T08:13:07Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2607_00588.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是一个被主流指标掩盖的连续 diffusion language model 失真问题：带 self-conditioning 的连续 DLM 在采样时会进入强重复的循环状态，而社区常用的 Gen-PPL 不但没有识别这个问题，反而会因为重复文本在外部 AR scorer 下概率更高而给出更低分。论文不是停留在“样本看起来重复”这一层，而是把问题定位到 self-conditioning 反馈回路的动力学：模型每一步把上一步的 clean estimate 再喂回去，形成一个收缩系统；在 ELF 这类模型里，这个系统沿某一个主方向存在一个近似一维的 contractive attractor，轨迹一旦被吸进去，就会朝着高度自预测、也就是高度重复的状态收敛。核心问题因此有两层：一是评价失真，二是训练后推理动力学中的结构性退化。","importance":"对预训练研究的意义很具体。第一，它直接质疑了连续 DLM 里把 Gen-PPL 当作 headline metric 的做法，说明低 perplexity 可能只是重复更严重，而不是语言建模更好。这会影响 diffusion/flow LM 与 AR LM 的比较口径。第二，它把一个生成缺陷还原成可分析的低维动力学问题，而不是把它当作 decode heuristic 可以修补的表面现象；这对新型 LM 范式很重要，因为很多非 AR 模型都依赖迭代 refinement 和自反馈。第三，它给出一个几乎不需要重训的干预方法 ACE，说明这类缺陷未必需要重新设计整个模型或目标函数，先把反馈回路中的不稳定方向找出来就能显著改善样本质量和有效采样成本。贡献量级上，它更像是对范式可靠性的校准工作：不是提出更强模型，而是指出现有结果里哪些结论站不住，以及应该怎样重新评估。","technical_approach":"技术路线分三步。第一步是把“低 Gen-PPL 伴随高重复”做成系统性证据，而不是个别样例。作者比较不同 ELF 尺度、不同采样器和不同 churn/feedback 设置，发现重复越重，Gen-PPL 往往越低；去掉重复片段后，模型排序会反转，小模型之所以看起来最好，主要是因为它最会重复。第二步是机制分析。作者把 self-conditioning 看成一个离散时间反馈系统：当前步的 denoiser 输出 clean estimate，下一步又把这个 estimate 作为条件输入。围绕采样轨迹线性化后，考察这个反馈映射的 Jacobian，发现最慢收缩的主模态几乎是一维，且与高重复轨迹高度对齐。再配合直接干预实验——单独增大 feedback strength 会同时提高重复率并降低 Gen-PPL——把因果链条补齐：不是重复偶然出现，而是反馈回路主动把轨迹拉向一个自预测 attractor。第三步是 ACE。它不改模型参数，也不加辅助分类器，而是在每一步 self-conditioning 输入上减去一个固定方向 d。这个方向通过无监督方式估计：收集高重复轨迹和低重复轨迹的反馈表示，取两组均值之差作为 attractor direction。作者还推导了一个 closed-form 的可用强度窗口，避免 steering 过强把正常语义也抹掉。关键点在于干预位置选在 latent feedback，而不是 token decode 端；论文的论证是，重复在 argmax readout 之前就已经在连续空间里形成，decode-time penalty 只能处理后果，碰不到成因。方法上这和常见 repetition penalty、n-gram blocking、temperature 调节都不同，也不同于重新训练一个 anti-repetition objective；它更接近对采样动力学做低维控制。","experiments":"实验部分最有信息量的是三类结果。第一类是指标失真。ELF-B 的原始 Gen-PPL 为 19.5，但去掉重复后升到 27.7；原本看起来更优的小模型，在去重后不再占优。样本级分析也显示，重复程度与 GPT-2 PPL 打分存在相关性，说明 scorer 确实偏爱这种退化文本。第二类是机制验证。调大 self-conditioning 反馈强度时，重复显著上升而 Gen-PPL 继续下降，支持“反馈制造重复、指标奖励重复”的链条。Jacobian 分析显示主导问题的是单一慢收缩方向；更尖锐的采样设置会让 basin 更深。第三类是 ACE 效果。作者用最小模型估计一个冻结方向，再迁移到不同采样超参和更大模型，和逐配置重估得到的方向余弦相似度在 0.82 到 0.96 DLM 的共性风险。引言里提到大量样本会锁死在少数 4-gram 上并循环数百词，附录表格给了更细的频次统计，这部分很值得看，因为它说明退化不是轻微重复，而是长程崩塌。","reading_guide":"建议先抓三条主线。第一条看第 3 节和相关表格，确认“Gen-PPL 会奖励重复”这件事到底有多严重。重点看模型排序在去重前后如何变化，以及 sample-level repetition 与 scorer PPL 的关系。第二条看第 4 节，这是全文最关键的部分。需要认真看 self-conditioning 的动力学重写、线性化分析、Jacobian 主方向、以及 feedback-strength ablation；如果只看结论不看这里，很容易把 ACE 误解成普通 steering trick。第三条看第 5 的推导和跨模型迁移结果。精读时可以继续追两个问题：一，这个一维 attractor 是训练目标诱导出来的，还是 self-conditioning inference recipe 放大的；二，若把 self-conditioning 在训练时做 dropout、随机化或正交约束，是否能从源头缓解问题。这篇文章没有完全回答，但给了很清楚的切入点。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper identifies a failure mode in self-conditioned continuous diffusion language models that is hidden by the field’s preferred metric. These models can generate heavily repetitive text, yet generative perplexity (Gen-PPL) often improves when repetition gets worse, because the external AR scorer assigns high probability to repeated sequences. The authors go beyond symptom-level observation and locate the mechanism in the self-conditioning loop itself. Each denoising step feeds its previous clean estimate back into the next step, creating a contractive dynamical system. In ELF-like models, this system develops an effectively one-dimensional attractor: once a trajectory aligns with that direction, it is pulled toward highly self-predictable, repetitive states. So the paper is solving two linked problems: metric mismeasurement and a structural inference-time degeneration in continuous DLMs.","importance":"This matters for pretraining research in a fairly direct way. First, it weakens the practice of treating Gen-PPL as a reliable headline metric for continuous diffusion or flow-based LMs. A lower score may simply mean stronger repetition, not better language modeling. That changes how one should compare non-AR models against AR baselines. Second, the paper turns a visible generation defect into a low-dimensional dynamical systems problem. That is useful beyond ELF, because many non-AR paradigms rely on iterative refinement and self-feedback. Third, it shows that the defect can be mitigated without retraining, by intervening on the feedback state rather than redesigning the whole model or objective. The contribution is less about building a stronger model and more about correcting the field’s understanding of what current results actually mean and how these models should be evaluated.","technical_approach":"$15","experiments":"$16","reading_guide":"$17","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"必须读：它会改变对 diffusion LM 指标的 prior，Gen-PPL 低不够用，应复现重复剥离和 ACE 方向分析。","en":"Must read: it changes the prior on diffusion-LM evaluation; low Gen-PPL is not enough, and the repetition-stripping plus ACE direction analysis should be reproduced."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Shuai Zhang","Zijie Chen","Hongliang He","Lun Du","Zhenzhong Lan"],"notable_authors":["Zhenzhong Lan"],"tags":["diffusion-LM","self-conditioning","evaluation","repetition","generation-dynamics"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00588","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.00588","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2607.00208","title":"SLIM-RL: Risk-Budgeted Random-Masking RL for Diffusion LLMs Without Trajectory Slicing","abstract":"Reinforcement learning for diffusion large language models (dLLMs) has largely moved to trajectory-aware methods. The current state of the art, TraceRL, holds that random masking is mismatched with the model's inference trajectory, and it reconstructs that trajectory during training by slicing each rollout into up to K/s trajectory-aligned training samples, a cost that grows with the block size K. We show that this mismatch can be mitigated without reconstructing the trajectory. Our method, SLIM","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-30T21:38:46Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2607_00208.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文处理 diffusion LLM 强化学习里的一个具体矛盾：trajectory-aware RL 通过复原模型推理轨迹来降低训练和推理的不匹配，但代价随 block size 增长；随机 mask 训练便宜，却容易产生高方差梯度，并且 rollout 阶段会因为逐点 confidence threshold 过度提交错误 token。","importance":"对预训练和后训练接口的启发在于，它把 diffusion LM 的 RL 问题拆成两个可控环节：rollout 质量和优化方差。结论比较明确：不一定要复原完整 denoising trajectory，随机 mask 目标在风险受控和方差降低后，可以达到甚至超过 trajectory slicing。对 dLLM 这类非 AR 建模范式很有参考意义，因为它说明后训练目标不必机械追随推理轨迹，关键是控制训练样本中的错误提交和梯度噪声。","technical_approach":"SLIM-RL 有三部分。第一，提出 τ-budget dynamic-unmasking decoder。传统 dynamic sampling 对每个位置单独判断 p_i 是否超过阈值 τ，容易在一次 step 中提交很多“刚刚过线”的 token。SLIM-RL 改成控制累计风险：只提交一组置信度最高的位置，使其累计不确定性 ∑(1-p_i) 不超过 m(1-τ)。这里 m 是本 step 计划提交的规模。这个规则训练免费，同时用于 rollout 和 inference。第二，优化阶段仍使用 trace-free random masking，但加入方差降低设计，包括 sequence-level length-normalized ratio、对 masking level 做 deterministic quadrature，以及使用 unnormalized advantage。第三，提出 mean-preserving、单调递减的 per-block mask schedule，把更多 policy-gradient 信号放到最早、上下文条件最充分的 block 上，而不是均匀随机 mask。和 TraceRL 的主要区别是：TraceRL 把一个 rollout 切成多份 trajectory-aligned samples；SLIM-RL 不切轨迹，而是通过 rollout 风险预算和优化方差控制来弥补 mismatch。","experiments":"主要实验在 SDAR-4B 和 SDAR-1.7B 上进行，比较对象包括
